### [v0.33.0](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases/tag/v0.33.0rc3)

**来源**: vLLM GitHub Releases

**摘要**: <h1>v0.33.0</h1>
<h2>Highlights</h2>
<p>This release features 927 commits from 389 contributors (122 new)!</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: Reflection Beam-1.0 (501B MoE, 23B active) with its DFlash2 drafter (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62074">#62074</a>), Yandex AliceAI-Foundation-80B-A3B (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57963">#57963</a>), Recursive Flint (40B MoE, 2.5B active) (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62938">#62938</a>), Muse Spark Open (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61879">#61879</a>), Step-5 Preview (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61727">#61727</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63147">#63147</a>), NVIDIA Alpamayo 2 Super (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62957">#62957</a>), Nemotron-Labs Dory1.0-4B (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60149">#60149</a>), Nemotron Parse 3.5 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61651">#61651</a>), MiniCPM-OCR 1.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63095">#63095</a>), Domyn Edge (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62544">#62544</a>) and the ColUltraVBERT visual document retriever (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62237">#62237</a>).</li>
<li><strong>PyTorch 2.14</strong>: vLLM is upgraded to PyTorch 2.14.0, with torchvision 0.29.0 and Triton 3.8.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52183">#52183</a>).</li>
<li><strong>CUDA 12.9 dropped</strong>: release wheels and images are CUDA 13.0 only. The CUDA 12.9 wheels and the <code>-cu129</code> / <code>-cu129-ubuntu2404</code> images are no longer published (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62575">#62575</a>); use the default CUDA 13.0 artifacts instead.</li>
<li><strong>libtorch ABI stable CUDA wheel</strong>: the CUDA wheel is now ABI stable with libtorch.</li>
<li><strong>AWS EFA image</strong>: a new <code>vllm/vllm-openai:v0.33.0-efa</code> image (x86_64 and aarch64) bundles the AWS EFA libraries and the EFA build of the Mooncake transfer engine, for KV transfer and multi-node serving on EFA-enabled EC2 instances (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58768">#58768</a>).</li>
<li><strong><code>vllm/vllm</code> image alias</strong>: every release image is now also published under the shorter <code>vllm/vllm</code> name, e.g. <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0</code>, <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0-efa</code> and <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0-rocm</code>.</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding</strong>: the new XPress refiner for block-diffusion drafters such as DFlash and DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54448">#54448</a>); FLy, an opt-in lossy verification policy with 1.04-1.31x decode throughput on MI355X and B300 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53987">#53987</a>); DFlash/DSpark no longer reserve draft slots in the token budget, taking DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash + DSpark on GB300 from 1373 to 3390 output tok/s in the PR's benchmark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59468">#59468</a>); quantized draft <code>lm_head</code> (<code>draft_lm_head_quantization</code>, +18% decode at concurrency 1) (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59973">#59973</a>); lookahead block hashes so EAGLE/MTP prefix-cache hits keep their last block, on by default (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61589">#61589</a>); and MTP drafts in the Transformers modeling backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61270">#61270</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Faster decode on NVIDIA</strong>: opt-in latency-specialized decode on GB300 for Kimi-K3 (1.5-2x at TP8/TP16, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61867">#61867</a>) and DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (18.3% faster decode step at batch 1, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61868">#61868</a>); DeepSeek-V4/V4.1 MXFP4 experts run as block FP8 through Humming on Hopper (+9-21% decode on 4x H200, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61875">#61875</a>); FA4 sparse MLA decode on Blackwell (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55866">#55866</a>); FlashInfer DCP prefill on trtllm-gen with 39-48% lower mean TTFT at 100k input on 8xB200 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62029">#62029</a>); and decode-only full CUDA graphs under MRV2 prefill context parallelism (2.6x over eager, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62377">#62377</a>).</li>
<li><strong>AMD ROCm</strong>: the default ROCm image and wheels move to ROCm 10.0, with ROCm 7.2 still published as <code>-rocm72</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58761">#58761</a>); MonoKernel decode via <code>--monokernel</code> for DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash on MI300X/MI325X (2.32x P50 interactivity at concurrency 1, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60731">#60731</a>) and on every MI355X layer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61082">#61082</a>), and for MiniMax-M3-MXFP4 on MI355X (16.6% lower TPOT, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62712">#62712</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62994">#62994</a>); the <code>ROCM_OCTAVE</code> 3-4 bit KV cache (4.6x the KV tokens of fp16 on RX 7900 XTX, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59774">#59774</a>); and gfx1250 enablement for gpt-oss-120b, DeepSeek-R1, DeepSeek-V4-Flash and MiniMax-M3 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58800">#58800</a>).</li>
<li><strong>API and serving</strong>: the OpenAI-compatible <code>/v1/decisions</code> endpoint, on by default for supported generation models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60465">#60465</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61550">#61550</a>); token embeddings with optional late chunking on <code>/v1/embeddings</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61772">#61772</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63051">#63051</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60164">#60164</a>); <code>output_mode="derender"</code> on <code>/inference/v1/generate</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60082">#60082</a>); a large Rust frontend parity push, including no more HTTP 500 on long conversations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62374">#62374</a>) and unsupported flags failing at startup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62431">#62431</a>); concurrent worker startup with TP &gt; 1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60028">#60028</a>); and a watchdog that dumps stack traces from stuck engine and worker processes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55700">#55700</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Breaking changes</strong>: Medusa and the MLP speculator removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61701">#61701</a>); the legacy Ray compiled-graph executor removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61712">#61712</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62228">#62228</a>); <code>--mamba-cache-mode</code> deprecated (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60043">#60043</a>); requests sent while generation is paused now get HTTP 503 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51488">#51488</a>); the NIXL connector needs NIXL 1.5 or newer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61032">#61032</a>); LM Format Enforcer and <code>--ir-op-priority</code> deprecated (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63423">#63423</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63371">#63371</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Release Artifacts</h2>

<h3>Python Wheels</h3>

<thead>
<tr>
<th>Platform</th>
<th>Install</th>
</tr>

</tr>

<p>Every image is also available under the shorter <code>vllm/vllm</code> name: <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0</code>, <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0-efa</code>, <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0-rocm</code> (<code>-rocm100</code>, <code>-rocm72</code>), <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0-cpu</code> and <code>vllm/vllm:v0.33.0-xpu</code>. <code>latest</code> works the same way, e.g. <code>vllm/vllm:latest-rocm</code>.</p>
<h3>Other Artifacts</h3>
<p>Pre-built release artifacts are available in the <strong>Assets</strong> section at the bottom of this page, including:</p>
<ul>
<li>Source distribution tarball</li>
<li>CUDA 13.0 Python wheels for x86_64 and arm64</li>
<li>CPU Python wheels for x86_64, arm64, and macOS</li>
<li>XPU Python wheel for x86_64</li>
</ul>
<h2>Model Support</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: Reflection Beam-1.0 (<code>BeamForCausalLM</code>) day-0 support with its DFlash2 drafter and fused Blackwell kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62074">#62074</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62042">#62042</a>), plus <code>beam</code> reasoning and tool parsers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62052">#62052</a>); Yandex AliceAI-Foundation-80B-A3B (Kimi Delta Attention hybrid, 512-expert MoE, 262K context) (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57963">#57963</a>); Recursive Flint BF16 and NVFP4 GGUF checkpoints with small-batch Blackwell decode kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62938">#62938</a>); Muse Spark Open (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61879">#61879</a>) with reasoning and tool parsers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62207">#62207</a>); Step-5 Preview (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61727">#61727</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62126">#62126</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62132">#62132</a>), running on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63147">#63147</a>) with LoRA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62498">#62498</a>); NVIDIA Alpamayo 2 Super (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62957">#62957</a>); Nemotron-Labs Dory1.0-4B (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60149">#60149</a>); Nemotron Parse 3.5 with an MTP proposer on MRV2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61651">#61651</a>); MiniCPM-OCR 1.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63095">#63095</a>); Domyn Edge (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62544">#62544</a>); ColUltraVBERT (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62237">#62237</a>); LFM2.5-Embedding-350M (<code>Lfm2BidirectionalModel</code>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62201">#62201</a>); Kolibri 1 through the Transformers backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60827">#60827</a>); and Decision 3.0 models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61565">#61565</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61778">#61778</a>) and Cloudflare Clef-Flash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61308">#61308</a>) on <code>/v1/systemone</code>.</li>
<li><strong>DeepSeek-V4 and V4.1</strong>: length-aware decode indexer block selection (about 850 us saved per step at 1M context, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61039">#61039</a>); MegaMoE experts loaded directly in the DeepGEMM layout so <code>reload_weights</code> works (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60096">#60096</a>); <code>fused_wqa_wkv</code> split by token across TP on SM90 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58872">#58872</a>); MegaAttn <code>wo_a</code> writes MXFP8 input for <code>wo_b</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60118">#60118</a>); native small-head FlashMLA sparse prefill on SM90 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61615">#61615</a>); FlashMLA interleaved KV layout (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61371">#61371</a>); redundant EPLB expert slots loaded (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59611">#59611</a>); GPU Engram tables read from peer HBM, fixing DEP decode hangs on GB200 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62091">#62091</a>); and fixes for Engram under PP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62208">#62208</a>), Engram all-to-all hangs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62269">#62269</a>), prefill index-workspace NaNs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60613">#60613</a>), C128 compressed-KV writes under PIECEWISE graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59709">#59709</a>), routed-expert rows under replay (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61643">#61643</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62652">#62652</a>) and the updated 1017 reference encoding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62199">#62199</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62200">#62200</a>).</li>
<li><strong>GLM</strong>: GLM-5.3-Flash defaults to an FP8 KV cache (9.8% higher end-to-end throughput in the PR's benchmark, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61228">#61228</a>) and gets mHC TileLang decode kernels on Blackwell (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62745">#62745</a>); GLM-5.3 per-token-group quant (2.8% ITL, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60927">#60927</a>), in-place draft decode metadata (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61873">#61873</a>) and skipped <code>concat_and_cache_mla</code> scales (1.2-3.0% TPOT on B300, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62102">#62102</a>); FP8 indexer WK pairs kept across <code>load_weights</code> calls, fixing thinking loops with runai_streamer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59223">#59223</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Kimi-K3</strong>: KDA RecoverSSM CuTe DSL verify/commit kernels on SM10x, with verify records moved out of the KV cache so KV connectors work (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63045">#63045</a>); online quantization of NVIDIA projections (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62980">#62980</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Qwen</strong>: Qwen3.8-Flash-Next FP8 QSA KV cache on Ampere (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60602">#60602</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60992">#60992</a>), packed NVFP4 PLE embeddings that fit Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-NVFP4 on one DGX Spark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56273">#56273</a>) and PLE short-conv prefill checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61601">#61601</a>); FlashInfer GDN prefill checkpoints for Qwen3-Next / Qwen3.5 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60050">#60050</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Other models</strong>: Cohere2MoE attention/MoE dual stream, single TP all-reduce and fused add + RMSNorm (1.14-1.36x TPOT, on by default, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62101">#62101</a>); Beam TP all-reduce fused with norms and MoE finalize (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62366">#62366</a>); MiniMax-M3 CUTLASS MSA decode crossover (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55838">#55838</a>); Granite MoE as an EAGLE3/DFlash/DSpark target (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61110">#61110</a>); NemotronHMTP sharing target embeddings (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62194">#62194</a>); LoRA for BERT cross-encoders and token classification (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61279">#61279</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61391">#61391</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal</strong>: PaddleOCR-VL batched vision encoder (34-85% more throughput, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61458">#61458</a>) and <code>compile_mm_encoder</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61838">#61838</a>); DeepSeek-VL2 ViT full CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46005">#46005</a>); Gemma4Unified batched embedding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60643">#60643</a>); Pixtral real-valued RoPE under compile (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63145">#63145</a>); and fixes for EXAONE-4.5 images (broken since v0.29.0, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61104">#61104</a>), Gemma 4 encoder graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61365">#61365</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60687">#60687</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63538">#63538</a>), DeepSeek-VL2 encoder cache and PP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63120">#63120</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63192">#63192</a>) and Gemma 4 placeholders (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61196">#61196</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Transformers modeling backend</strong>: MTP speculative drafts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61270">#61270</a>), fused post-top-k MoE routers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57410">#57410</a>), graph caching instead of AOT caching (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58918">#58918</a>) and dynamic-RoPE CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58064">#58064</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Engine Core</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding</strong>: XPress (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54448">#54448</a>), FLy (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53987">#53987</a>), no draft-slot budget reservation for DFlash/DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59468">#59468</a>), quantized draft <code>lm_head</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59973">#59973</a>), lookahead block hashes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61589">#61589</a>), MRV2 skipping drafts no request can use (up to 7.3% prefill throughput with DFlash, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62784">#62784</a>), Triton split-K verify (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56148">#56148</a>), varlen Mamba2 decode for adaptive verification (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53036">#53036</a>), and fixes for stop-trimmed token counts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58668">#58668</a>), block verification randomness (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61161">#61161</a>), MTP block tables (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61174">#61174</a>), grammar masks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52477">#52477</a>), EAGLE SWA prefix hits (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62206">#62206</a>), DFlash/DSpark Mamba checkpoint reuse (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54163">#54163</a>) and PP warmup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62129">#62129</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Model Runner V2</strong>: batch-sharded sampling for GPT-OSS (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61257">#61257</a>) with adaptive verification (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58466">#58466</a>), 31-120x faster long-prompt staged writes on GB200 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62698">#62698</a>), encoder towers skipped on later PP stages (0.58-1.27 GiB per stage, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60238">#60238</a>), async scheduling kept for ngram (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62521">#62521</a>), <code>input_ids</code> on later PP stages (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61016">#61016</a>), and memory-profiling fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57926">#57926</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58243">#58243</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61509">#61509</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63431">#63431</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Sampling</strong>: Triton top-k/top-p skips the cutoff search for masked logits (up to about 8x, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61213">#61213</a>); small-batch split top-p (1.22-2.70x on B300, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61996">#61996</a>); and fixes for huge logits and boundary ties (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58231">#58231</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62046">#62046</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Startup and reliability</strong>: concurrent local worker startup with TP &gt; 1 (about 50s saved at TP8, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60028">#60028</a>); shared Triton JIT compile pool (9-28% faster cold warmup, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61422">#61422</a>); engine and worker watchdog (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55700">#55700</a>); libgomp fork deadlock fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62884">#62884</a>); Fast Start with expert parallelism (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55490">#55490</a>) and daemon fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62583">#62583</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63451">#63451</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63088">#63088</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62734">#62734</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63506">#63506</a>); and SHM broadcast reader memory growth fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63165">#63165</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Memory and performance</strong>: UVA-offloaded MoE experts on the sorted fused-MoE path (+7-19%, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62054">#62054</a>); DispatchPooler bypass for homogeneous pooling (up to 10.3%, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62940">#62940</a>); linear-time encoder cache free (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62850">#62850</a>); sliding-window block tables null freed entries (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60767">#60767</a>).</li>
<li><strong>LoRA</strong>: expert LoRA for MXFP4 MoE on <code>flashinfer_trtllm</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61979">#61979</a>), text-only LoRAs on multimodal wrappers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62224">#62224</a>), unsupported models rejected at validation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61884">#61884</a>), and fixes for <code>load_inplace</code> reloads (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/41482">#41482</a>), <code>max_loras</code> accounting (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60306">#60306</a>), SM90 <code>lora_expand</code> NaNs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61906">#61906</a>) and wake-up from level-2 sleep (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62358">#62358</a>).</li>
<li><strong>RL</strong>: per-choice <code>return_weight_versions</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61347">#61347</a>), NCCL M2N transfer with per-parameter meshes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60934">#60934</a>), parameter metadata sent once (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/35911">#35911</a>), the <code>ParamMeta</code> / <code>iter_full()</code> / <code>iter_local()</code> weight-transfer API (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63256">#63256</a>), and a silent MoE weight corruption fix with packed NCCL transfer plus layerwise reload (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62555">#62555</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Batch invariance</strong>: compressed-tensors W4A16/W8A16 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60524">#60524</a>), GateLinear routers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59985">#59985</a>), online FP8 MoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57092">#57092</a>), and inductor batch-invariant mode under <code>VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61118">#61118</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Large Scale Serving</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Context parallelism</strong>: MRV2 PCP decode full CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62377">#62377</a>); PCP for DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash with prefix caching and SWA bounded replay (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59857">#59857</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62183">#62183</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62384">#62384</a>); FlashInfer DCP prefill on trtllm-gen (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62029">#62029</a>); direct DCP all-to-all with strided LSE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60373">#60373</a>); FlashMLA sparse on the a2a DCP backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61285">#61285</a>).</li>
<li><strong>HiSparse</strong>: direct reads of GPU-resident KV, fixing OOM with long prompts and a 3.3x slowdown on 1M-token prefill (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60417">#60417</a>); faster host pool prefault (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60869">#60869</a>); and P/D correctness fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61659">#61659</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62739">#62739</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63827">#63827</a>).</li>
<li><strong>KV connectors</strong>: NIXL 1.5 strided descriptors (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61032">#61032</a>), sleep mode with the pull connector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59635">#59635</a>), mixed hybrid MLA block sizes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61002">#61002</a>) and GQA draft head sharding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58471">#58471</a>); Mooncake sleep mode over RDMA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59625">#59625</a>) and Prometheus metrics (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56939">#56939</a>); MoRIIO hybrid and heterogeneous-TP WRITE transfers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60544">#60544</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61726">#61726</a>); streaming responses carry <code>kv_transfer_params</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/40186">#40186</a>); fault tolerance extended to P/D, fixing KV block leaks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56430">#56430</a>).</li>
<li><strong>KV offload</strong>: CPU offload over up to 32 shm files (GLM-5.3 offload startup 63.6 s to 38.3 s, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59546">#59546</a>), opt-in same-step small loads (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61961">#61961</a>), EAGLE/MTP partial tails in align mode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61019">#61019</a>), lock/unlock and off-thread tiering (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58168">#58168</a>), and many correctness fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63266">#63266</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60810">#60810</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63652">#63652</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62929">#62929</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63010">#63010</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60574">#60574</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63655">#63655</a>).</li>
<li><strong>MoE and DP</strong>: DeepEP-v2 fused decode globalize+align (up to 2.01x on H200 EP32, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53911">#53911</a>); Elastic EP with an external load balancer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43202">#43202</a>); low-latency CPU all-reduce for DP syncs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61767">#61767</a>) and a DP wave hang fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59559">#59559</a>); EPLB fixes for MXFP4/MXFP8 scales (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61786">#61786</a>) and NIXL reads (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62081">#62081</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Hardware &amp; Performance</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>NVIDIA</strong>: Kimi-K3 and DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash latency-specialized decode on GB300 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61867">#61867</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61868">#61868</a>); FA4 sparse MLA decode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55866">#55866</a>); opt-in CuTe DSL MLA context prefill (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62069">#62069</a>); DeepGEMM MegaMoE with NVFP4 routed experts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61538">#61538</a>) and warmed-up kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60468">#60468</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61657">#61657</a>); Triton GDN decode tuned on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61355">#61355</a>); GLM-5.2/5.3 low-latency BF16 GEMM on SM107 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62843">#62843</a>); pre-SM90 FlashInfer spec decode in full CUDA graphs (73.6 to 108.1 tok/s on SM80, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60532">#60532</a>); <code>cooperative_topk</code> sized by row length (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62171">#62171</a>); fused GDN MTP decode for SM80-SM90 in CUDA 12.x builds (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60896">#60896</a>); and DGX Spark Qwen4Exp TP2 plans (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61437">#61437</a>).</li>
<li><strong>AMD ROCm</strong>: ROCm 10.0 default image (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58761">#58761</a>); MonoKernel decode for DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash and V4-Pro and MiniMax-M3 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60731">#60731</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61082">#61082</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61482">#61482</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62712">#62712</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62994">#62994</a>); <code>ROCM_OCTAVE</code> KV cache (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59774">#59774</a>); gfx1250 enablement (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58800">#58800</a>); DeepSeek-V4 MXFP4 indexer K cache by default on CDNA4 (up to 30.7% lower TTFT, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60834">#60834</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63669">#63669</a>); AITER MegaMoE fixed-slot dispatch (14-15%, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61262">#61262</a>) and shared-expert overlap (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61819">#61819</a>); Kimi-K3 Gluon KDA kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57392">#57392</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59552">#59552</a>) and specialized decode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60683">#60683</a>); Qwen3.5 fused GDN MTP decode (+10-16% on MI350X, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61855">#61855</a>); Qwen3.8-Flash-Next native W4A4 (1.9-2.3x, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60816">#60816</a>); AITER top-k for sparse indexers on gfx950 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61943">#61943</a>); FlyDSL GDN prefill default on gfx942/gfx950 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60645">#60645</a>); RDNA3 TRITON_ATTN addressing restored (about 18%, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60084">#60084</a>); and a MiniMax-M3-MXFP4 startup fix for very large GEMMs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64567">#64567</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Intel XPU</strong>: SYCL GDN prefill backend selection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56835">#56835</a>), torch MXFP4 linear backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51965">#51965</a>), SYCL FlashAttention for prefix-LM text batches (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62014">#62014</a>) and NIXL 1.5.0 in the image (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60356">#60356</a>).</li>
<li><strong>CPU</strong>: zentorch 2.14.0.1 with float16 on Zen CPUs with AVX512-FP16 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61411">#61411</a>); MRV2 Triton warmup at startup removing an 11-20 s first-request compile (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62888">#62888</a>); INT8 W8A8 MoE brgemm (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60740">#60740</a>); hybrid-model prefix caching (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61805">#61805</a>); and Run:ai Model Streamer in the CPU image (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61382">#61382</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Other</strong>: tpu-inference v0.32.0 with torch 2.14.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63080">#63080</a>) and more out-of-tree platform hooks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62026">#62026</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63017">#63017</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63601">#63601</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63457">#63457</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Quantization</h2>
<ul>
<li>Humming runs DeepSeek-V4/V4.1 MXFP4 experts as block FP8 on Hopper (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61875">#61875</a>) and honors <code>--quantization-config.moe.activation</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62751">#62751</a>); pluggable KV cache data types (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52670">#52670</a>); online re-quantization extended to NVFP4 MoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60276">#60276</a>); Quark MXFP8 routed experts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63125">#63125</a>); MoNe expert-pruning checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50415">#50415</a>); torch MXFP8 linear backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51933">#51933</a>); FP8 per-token-group quant 1.15-1.29x faster (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61343">#61343</a>); Cosmos3-Edge ModelOpt FP8 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57393">#57393</a>); and a Nemotron-3-Nano NVFP4 garbage-output fix under torch.compile (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61749">#61749</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>API &amp; Frontend</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New endpoints and options</strong>: <code>/v1/decisions</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60465">#60465</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61550">#61550</a>); token embeddings and late chunking (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61772">#61772</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63051">#63051</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60164">#60164</a>); <code>output_mode="derender"</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60082">#60082</a>); <code>--reasoning-effort-map</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61094">#61094</a>); <code>--sleep-mode-backend</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63138">#63138</a>); dynamic profiler options on <code>/start_profile</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51974">#51974</a>); images on <code>/v1/systemone</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62181">#62181</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62437">#62437</a>); Responses <code>max_tool_calls</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47112">#47112</a>) and prompt-cache fields (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62856">#62856</a>); and <code>/openapi.json</code> response schemas (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60926">#60926</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Tool calling and reasoning</strong>: linear-time streaming tool arguments for GLM, Gemma 4, Qwen3 XML (2.1 s to 11 ms for a 12 KB call) and MiniMax M2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61253">#61253</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61494">#61494</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62027">#62027</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62035">#62035</a>); DeepSeek V3 multi-line arguments (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60362">#60362</a>); structured output masks grammar-invalid reasoning ends instead of returning 500 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61574">#61574</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Behavior</strong>: chat <code>finish_reason</code> and Responses <code>status</code> reflect what happened (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59324">#59324</a>); beam search normalizes by generated length so <code>length_penalty</code> applies (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62928">#62928</a>); automatic truncation reserves one generation token (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62010">#62010</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Rust frontend</strong>: <code>--trust-request-chat-template</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62673">#62673</a>), <code>--return-tokens-as-token-ids</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63318">#63318</a>), <code>--tokenizer</code> options (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62416">#62416</a>), <code>--custom-histogram-buckets</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62826">#62826</a>) and per-request metrics (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59314">#59314</a>); request and response parity with the Python frontend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62434">#62434</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62425">#62425</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62426">#62426</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62427">#62427</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62428">#62428</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61949">#61949</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62429">#62429</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62822">#62822</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62663">#62663</a>); and the MiniJinja fuel limit only on request-supplied templates (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62374">#62374</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Security</h2>
<ul>
<li>Per-request budgets for downloaded and decoded media bytes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61226">#61226</a>); speculators checkpoint metadata can no longer reference local verifier paths (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61587">#61587</a>); <code>certifi&gt;=2026.10.19</code> and refreshed CA certificates (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62868">#62868</a>); raised floors for aiohttp, Pillow, MCP and multidict (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62835">#62835</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Dependencies</h2>
<ul>
<li>PyTorch 2.14.0, torchvision 0.29.0, Triton 3.8.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52183">#52183</a>).</li>
<li>FlashInfer 0.7.2.post1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60948">#60948</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61913">#61913</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63466">#63466</a>), humming-kernels 0.1.19 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62816">#62816</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64802">#64802</a>), DeepGEMM 2.8.1 and later pins (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61149">#61149</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61744">#61744</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63253">#63253</a>), FlashMLA 2026.09.30 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61371">#61371</a>), TokenSpeed MLA 2.0.3 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61125">#61125</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61689">#61689</a>), <code>nvidia-cudnn-frontend&gt;=1.31.0</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61465">#61465</a>), NIXL 1.5.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60428">#60428</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60356">#60356</a>) and LMCache 0.5.6 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62835">#62835</a>).</li>
<li>ROCm: AITER v0.1.26 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61697">#61697</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62486">#62486</a>) and UCX v1.23.0-rc1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62827">#62827</a>). CPU: zentorch 2.14.0.1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61411">#61411</a>). TPU: tpu-inference v0.32.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63080">#63080</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Build</h2>
<ul>
<li>Release wheels and images are CUDA 13.0 only (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62575">#62575</a>); new <code>vllm-openai-efa</code> Docker target (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58768">#58768</a>); ROCm 10.0 is the default ROCm image with ROCm 7.2 as <code>-rocm72</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58761">#58761</a>); ROCm wheel builds about 4-7x faster (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63105">#63105</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Breaking Changes &amp; Deprecations</h2>
<ul>
<li>CUDA 12.9 wheels and images are no longer published (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62575">#62575</a>).</li>
<li>Medusa and the MLP speculator were removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61701">#61701</a>). The legacy Ray compiled-graph executor was removed and <code>VLLM_USE_RAY_V2_EXECUTOR_BACKEND</code> is gone; <code>RayExecutorV2</code> is the only Ray executor (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61712">#61712</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62228">#62228</a>).</li>
<li>Deprecated: <code>--mamba-cache-mode</code> (no effect, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60043">#60043</a>), <code>--ir-op-priority</code> (removal in v0.34.0, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63371">#63371</a>), and the LM Format Enforcer structured-output backend (opt-in in v0.34.0, removal in v0.35.0, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63423">#63423</a>).</li>
<li>API changes: requests sent while generation is paused return HTTP 503 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51488">#51488</a>); <code>kv_transfer_params</code> / <code>ec_transfer_params</code> moved to the top level of render output (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61241">#61241</a>); LoRA on a model without LoRA support fails at validation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61884">#61884</a>); the RL weight-transfer API now uses <code>ParamMeta</code>, <code>iter_full()</code> and <code>iter_local()</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63256">#63256</a>); beam search scores are normalized by generated length (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62928">#62928</a>).</li>
<li>Rust frontend: unsupported <code>vllm serve</code> flags and request fields fail instead of being ignored (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62431">#62431</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62434">#62434</a>); request-supplied chat templates need <code>--trust-request-chat-template</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62673">#62673</a>); an empty <code>tools</code> array returns 400 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63060">#63060</a>).</li>
<li>The NIXL connector requires NIXL 1.5 or newer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61032">#61032</a>).</li>
<li>Removed env vars: <code>VLLM_ROCM_AITER_MLA_DCP_VERIFY</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61628">#61628</a>), <code>VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_FP4_ASM_GEMM</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61278">#61278</a>) and <code>VLLM_ROCM_AITER_MEGA_MOE_FUSE_SHARED_EXPERTS</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61345">#61345</a>); <code>VLLM_ROCM_MONO_DECODE</code> is replaced by <code>--monokernel</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61082">#61082</a>).</li>
<li>New defaults: GLM-5.3-Flash FP8 KV cache (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61228">#61228</a>); lookahead block hashes for EAGLE/MTP prefix caching (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61589">#61589</a>); Cohere2MoE dual stream (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62101">#62101</a>); FA4 sparse MLA decode at 16 or fewer query heads per rank on Blackwell (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55866">#55866</a>); ROCm: AITER FP8/FP4 BMM for MLA off (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53123">#53123</a>), FlyDSL GDN prefill (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60645">#60645</a>), AITER <code>topk_gating</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56226">#56226</a>), <code>rms_norm_gated</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60250">#60250</a>) and MXFP4 indexer K cache on CDNA4 for DeepSeek-V4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63669">#63669</a>).</li>
</ul>

### [CachyOS November 2026 release](https://lwn.net/Articles/1109269/)

**来源**: LWN.net (Linux Weekly News)

**摘要**: <p><a href="https://cachyos.org/">CachyOS</a>, a performance-focused
distribution based on <a href="https://archlinux.org/">Arch Linux</a>, has
announced its <a href="https://cachyos.org/blog/2611-november-release/">November&#160;2026</a>
release. Notable changes in this release include a new recovery environment
("cachyos-recovery") for rolling back broken systems, Easy Anti-Cheat (EAC) v2
support for games that require EAC to run, version 2.0.0 of the CachyOS Package
Installer, and more.</p>

**来源**: LWN.net (Linux Weekly News)

**摘要**: <p>Greg Kroah-Hartman has announced the <a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1108910/">7.3.6</a>,
<a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1108911/">6.18.65</a>,
<a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1108912/">6.12.119</a>,
<a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1108913/">6.6.163</a>,
<a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1108914/">6.1.193</a>,
<a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1109274/">5.15.226</a>, and
<a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1108915/">5.10.275</a> stable kernels. Each of these contains
quite a few patches throughout the tree&mdash;6.12.119 has the fewest with more
than 1,700 patches, while 6.6.163 has more than 3,300. Users are advised to
upgrade.</p>

## Performance

### [Seeing the machine code of your Java program, with a few lines of Java](https://lemire.me/blog/2026/11/23/seeing-the-machine-code-of-your-java-program/)

**来源**: Daniel Lemire (SIMD/Performance)

**摘要**: <img alt="" class="webfeedsFeaturedVisual wp-post-image" height="150" src="https://lemire.me/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/instructive-cover-150x150.jpg" style="display: block; margin-bottom: 5px; clear: both;" width="150" />When I want to know why some Java code is slow, I usually reach for JMH. JMH is great to time a function. But a timing tells you how slow the code is, not why. For that, you need to see the machine code that the JIT compiler produced, and where the processor spends its &#8230; <a class="more-link" href="https://lemire.me/blog/2026/11/23/seeing-the-machine-code-of-your-java-program/">Continue reading <span class="screen-reader-text">Seeing the machine code of your Java program, with a few lines of Java</span></a>

## Systems

### [Replacing the old battery on rechargeable bike lights](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/09/27/replacing-the-old-battery-on-rechargeable-bike-lights/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! Recently I needed bike lights for my bike. And I remembered that I
already had rechargeable bike lights that I bought ten years ago, that I hadn&rsquo;t
tried in a long time. I tried to recharge them, but after fully charging them,
they only worked for maybe 5 minutes before they turned off again.</p>
<p>I don&rsquo;t know much about electronics, but I&rsquo;ve been curious about whether it&rsquo;s
possible to fix old electronics for a long time, and this seemed like the
perfect repair project because I might just need to replace the battery.</p>
<p>So I went to the local queer makerspace where I&rsquo;m a member to use the soldering
iron and try to do it! I don&rsquo;t know much about electronics and this post does
not contain any safety advice because I don&rsquo;t know much about safety.
I think it&rsquo;s nice to do projects in a community space where you can get help.</p>
<h3 id="step-1-cut-it-open">step 1: cut it open</h3>
<p>The bike light felt like it was made of silicone, so I cut open the silicone in
a haphazard way along something that vaguely looked like a seam.</p>
<p>I definitely ripped some silicone in the process and it was pretty messy but I
got it open and found the circuit board.</p>
<p>I don&rsquo;t know the model number of the bike lights but there&rsquo;s a photo of them at
the end of the post.</p>
<h3 id="step-2-remove-the-screws">step 2: remove the screws</h3>
<p>There were some screws attaching things together so I removed them so I could
get the circuit board out.</p>
<p>Mostly I tried to remove as few screws as possible because I was worried about
losing them or not being able to put them back after. I probably put the screws
in a bag or something.</p>
<h3 id="step-3-get-the-circuit-board-out">step 3: get the circuit board out</h3>
<p>I took out the circuit board. Here&rsquo;s what it looked like:</p>
<img src="https://jvns.ca/images/pcb.png" />
<p>You can see where the battery is attached, I think it&rsquo;s left of RI3 and above
Q2.</p>
<p>Here&rsquo;s what the battery looked like:</p>
<img src="https://jvns.ca/images/battery.png" />
<h3 id="step-4-desolder-the-battery">step 4: desolder the battery</h3>
<p>I&rsquo;d never desoldered anything before, so I found
<a href="https://www.ifixit.com/Guide/How+To+Solder+and+Desolder+Connections/750#s4052">the iFixit guide to desoldering</a> and read it.
Also I asked my friends Lee and Lauria for advice.</p>
<p>Here were the steps I ended up following based on the guide &amp; the advice I got:</p>
<ol>
<li>Use a desoldering pump to remove most of the solder</li>
<li>Once most of it is gone, kind of pull them apart to try to separate them</li>
<li>Also try to avoid getting the battery too hot in the process by taking
breaks to let it cool down. I&rsquo;m not very good with a soldering iron
so it took a while.</li>
<li>The battery has an attachment that is welded to the top.
For a while I thought I needed to remove this and it seemed impossible, but
it turned out the replacement battery comes with that part so actually I was
supposed to leave it alone.</li>
</ol>
<h3 id="step-5-identify-the-battery">step 5: identify the battery</h3>
<p>In the picture of the battery in Step 3, you can see it says something like &ldquo;3&rdquo;
and &ldquo;LI???77&rdquo;. There&rsquo;s a piece of metal that I think is welded or something to
the top of the battery. It seemed impossible and also maybe not smart to try to
remove so I wasn&rsquo;t sure how to find out what an &ldquo;LI????77&rdquo; was or how to order
another one.</p>
<p>I&rsquo;ve been trying to avoid using LLMs (though I will not get into that because
I am exhausted by LLM discourse and I&rsquo;m sure you are too), but I really had
no idea how to figure out what the battery was so I asked an LLM. It gave the
response &ldquo;LIR2477&rdquo;, which (when I looked it up) looked exactly the same as my
battery so I figured that was plausible.</p>
<p>I would be interested to learn non-LLM ways to figure this out though.
There must be a way. Lauria showed me how to use
<a href="https://www.digikey.ca/en/products">DigiKey&rsquo;s search</a> which was very cool
though DigiKey didn&rsquo;t have that part.</p>
<p>(edit: someone in the replies told me that this kind of coin cell battery is
named according to its dimensions, and you can use plastic calipers to measure
the dimensions of the battery. So I guess a non-LLM way would be to measure
the battery with calipers and try to match it to something on the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_battery_sizes">List of battery sizes</a>
Wikipedia page, though that page only mentions CR 2477 and not LIR 2477. It&rsquo;s
a good example of what&rsquo;s fun for me about trying to avoid LLMs, this &ldquo;List of
battery sizes&rdquo; page is super interesting and if I use an LLM I might never find
it)</p>
<h3 id="step-6-buy-the-battery">step 6: buy the battery</h3>
<p>I went to AliExpress and ordered:</p>
<ol>
<li>2 batteries (I had 2 bike lights and I wanted to fix them both)</li>
<li>some silicone glue to glue things back together</li>
</ol>
<p>I think the batteries were $3 each and the glue was $8.</p>
<h3 id="step-7-solder-the-new-batteries-in-and-glue-it-back-together">step 7: solder the new batteries in and glue it back together</h3>
<p>The parts took maybe 2 weeks to arive, and once they arrived, I went back to the
makerspace and:</p>
<ul>
<li>soldered in the new batteries</li>
<li>put the screws back in. The screws were very small and hard to hold, so at
this point I dropped some screws on the ground and couldn&rsquo;t find them because
they were too small. So I just used fewer screws and hoped for the best.</li>
<li>used the glue to try to put everything back together.</li>
<li>Make a somewhat halfhearted attempt to clamp the parts I was gluing together</li>
</ul>
<p>Then after waiting some amount of time for the glue to dry I took it home and
waited 24 hours for the glue to cure.</p>
<p>Also I took the old batteries to somewhere nearby that accepts old batteries.</p>
<h3 id="it-works">it works!</h3>
<p>The lights work! I have used them to bike at night! I still haven&rsquo;t needed to
recharge them (and tragically I had to order a new Mini USB cable because I got
rid of all my Mini USB cables, so I&rsquo;m still waiting for that), so I still don&rsquo;t
know for sure how long the lifetime of the new battery will be.</p>
<p>Here&rsquo;s what the light looks like after re-gluing.
You can see that I didn&rsquo;t glue very carefully. It didn&rsquo;t really go back
together that well but I&rsquo;m hoping it&rsquo;ll be good enough.</p>
<img src="https://jvns.ca/images/light-after-1.png" />
<img src="https://jvns.ca/images/light-after-2.png" />
<p>I thought it was really cool that I was able to do this with extremely minimal
electronics skills! It cost about $20 CAD to buy the parts, and (whether or not
the repair holds up, I&rsquo;ll try to update this post in the future!), it was fun to
try to repair something and learn something new.</p>

---

### [Some more things about Django I've been enjoying](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/07/21/more-nice-django-things/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! I&rsquo;m on a funny journey right now where I&rsquo;m trying to learn how to make
websites in a sort of 2010 style, where I have an SQL database and render some
HTML on the backend.</p>
<p>It&rsquo;s kind of an interesting journey because it doesn&rsquo;t necessarily feel &ldquo;easy&rdquo;
to me to make websites in this way: I never learned how to do it in the 2000s or
2010s, and there&rsquo;s a lot I need to learn.</p>
<p>So here are some Django features that make building this kind of site feel
more achievable than when I was trying and failing to use Go&rsquo;s standard library
or Flask. And I&rsquo;ll talk about a couple of issues with Django I&rsquo;ve run into.</p>
<h3 id="why-learn-to-make-websites-like-it-s-2010">why learn to make websites like it&rsquo;s 2010?</h3>
<p>Previously the toolkit I felt confident with for making websites was:</p>
<ul>
<li>static site generators (like for this blog)</li>
<li>static sites that do some fun stuff with Javascript (like this <a href="https://sql-playground.wizardzines.com/">sql playground</a>)</li>
<li>simple Vue.js single page apps with either a Lambda as a backend or a Go backend (like <a href="https://messwithdns.net/">mess with dns</a>)</li>
</ul>
<p>I really liked this frontend-heavy approach for these super simple applications
but when I started thinking about making something with a lot of different pages
(instead of literally just one page), I didn&rsquo;t feel so excited about the options
I saw that involved a lot of frontend code. So I figured I&rsquo;d try the backend.</p>
<p>Writing a backend-focused site that uses as little JS as possible feels
the same to me in a way as writing a single-page JS website that does as little
on the backend as possible, even though they might seem like opposites. In both
cases I&rsquo;m just trying to keep as much of the logic as possible in one place.</p>
<p>Now for some thoughts about Django!</p>
<h3 id="i-m-enjoying-query-builders">I&rsquo;m enjoying query builders</h3>
<p>I learned that I can define a &ldquo;query set&rdquo; class in Django with a bunch of
methods with different <code>WHERE</code> statements I might want to use while constructing
a query:</p>
<p>Here&rsquo;s how I use it in my view code once I&rsquo;ve defined what all the methods mean:</p>
<pre><code>Events.objects.approved()
  .for_tab(tab)
  .with_festivals(tab_params.festival_slugs)
  .is_free(tab_params.free)
  .is_outdoors(tab_params.outdoors)
</code></pre>
<p>and here&rsquo;s how I define the methods:</p>
<pre><code>class EventQuerySet(SearchableQuerySetMixin, models.QuerySet):
    def approved(self):
        return self.filter(approved_at__isnull=False)

    def future(self):
        today = timezone.localdate()
        return self.filter(end__gt=self._midnight(today))

    def with_tags(self, tags):
        if tags:
            return self.filter(tags__name__in=tags).distinct()
        return self
</code></pre>
<p>The syntax for defining the filters isn&rsquo;t my favourite, but I spend most of my
time just using the methods, and it feels super readable and nice to use, and
it makes me want to look into other query builder libraries in the future. In
the past I thought &ldquo;I know SQL, who needs a query builder?&rdquo;, but this kind of
structure does make it really nice to read.</p>
<p>I found an example of someone who <a href="https://github.com/lemon24/reader/blob/15121f667a6f2e388f0072a3fcd715f533883899/src/reader/_sql_utils.py">wrote their own small query builder in Python</a>
that I want to read later to think about whether I would enjoy using a more
minimal version of this.</p>
<h3 id="the-template-filters-are-awesome">the template filters are awesome</h3>
<p>There are a bunch of little quality of life
<a href="https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/templates/builtins/">filters available in Django templates</a> that are super useful for generating HTML. The ones I&rsquo;ve used so far are:</p>
<ul>
<li>translating plain text URLs into links, or line breaks into <code>&lt;br&gt;</code> (<code>{{ event.description|urlize|linebreaksbr }} </code>)</li>
<li>formatting dates (<code>{{ row.date|date:&quot;M j&quot; }}</code>)</li>
<li><code>json_script</code>, which takes a Python dictionary and automatically converts it to JSON and inserts it into the HTML as a <code>&lt;script&gt;</code> tag in a safe way</li>
</ul>
<p>These are all small things individually but I feel like it makes a big difference somehow to just have them available.</p>
<h3 id="querystring-is-cool"><code>querystring</code> is cool</h3>
<p>I think my favourite template filter is <code>querystring</code>: in this site sometimes
we use filters like <code>?date=2026-06-01</code> to decide what&rsquo;s displayed. <code>querystring</code>
that will make a link to the same query string with one change, like this to
link to the previous date:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;a href=&quot;{% querystring date=nav.prev_date%}&quot;&gt;
</code></pre>
<p>Or to remove the <code>outdoors</code> parameter:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;a href=&quot;{% querystring outdoors=None %}&quot;&gt;
</code></pre>
<h3 id="automatic-database-migrations-are-still-great">automatic database migrations are still great</h3>
<p>I still really love Django&rsquo;s automatic database system. It&rsquo;s amazing to be
able to just edit a model to add a new field or whatever, and then Django
automatically generates the migration.</p>
<p>So far we have done 19 database migrations and I think there will probably be
more! It makes a huge difference for me to be able to just easily change the
database as my understanding of the problem changes.</p>
<h3 id="i-do-not-want-to-organize-my-code-with-inheritance">I do not want to organize my code with inheritance</h3>
<p>Django&rsquo;s documentation sometimes offers the option of using class-based views
and inheritance to organize the code in your views. For example I have four
views that share a lot of code, and I could use inheritance to manage that by
defining some kind of parent class and then having my other views inherit from
it.</p>
<p>I tried it out and I did not enjoy the experience of using inheritance to
share code between views. I switched to using functions instead, sort of how
<a href="https://spookylukey.github.io/django-views-the-right-way/">this post</a> advocates, and that was a lot more straightforward.
I&rsquo;ve never had a good experience using inheritance in Python and I don&rsquo;t think
I&rsquo;ll try to use it again.</p>
<p>But I don&rsquo;t mind using inheritance to use the interfaces Django itself provides: for
example if I want to define a query set I need to write something like
<code>class EventQuerySet(SearchableQuerySetMixin, models.QuerySet)</code>.
I don&rsquo;t think too hard about it and it seems to work.</p>
<p>(as a meta comment: I&rsquo;ve been working on talking about my programming opinions
by just saying &ldquo;THING does not feel good to me, I prefer OTHER THING instead&rdquo;.
That post I linked to says that function-based views are the &ldquo;right way&rdquo;. I&rsquo;m
not very invested in whether it&rsquo;s &ldquo;right&rdquo;, but it&rsquo;s validating to know that
other people feel similarly to me about inheritance)</p>
<h3 id="i-don-t-know-how-to-think-about-django-performance">I don&rsquo;t know how to think about Django performance</h3>
<p>At some point the LLM scrapers discovered our site, and started sending us maybe 10
requests per second. I blocked them which is working for now, but it made me
think about what the site&rsquo;s capacity is. I&rsquo;m used to writing Go backends where
the performance situation is pretty straightforward (usually everything is just
fast enough), and a Django site is very different.</p>
<p>Some light load testing (with (<code>ab -n 1000 -c 1</code>) shows that right now we can
serve about 2-3 requests per second (on a ~$10/month VM).</p>
<p>It&rsquo;s tempting for me to go down a rabbit hole where I do a bunch of profiling to
figure out what&rsquo;s slow and try to make it faster
(there&rsquo;s <a href="https://github.com/benfred/py-spy">py-spy</a> for that, and py-spy is great and super easy to use, and profiling is fun!)
But I really don&rsquo;t understand what I should expect in terms of performance from
a Django site and how I should be thinking about at a higher level.</p>
<p>Some things I haven&rsquo;t figured out yet:</p>
<ul>
<li>If I have a site that&rsquo;s going to be getting occasional bursts of traffic, do I want to be able to scale up?</li>
<li>Do I want to design the site so that more things can be cached? (and do I really have to? caches are so annoying to get right!)</li>
<li>The <a href="https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/topics/performance/">django performance docs</a> say that Jinja is faster for templating, do I want to think about switching templating systems?</li>
<li>Those docs also say &ldquo;{% block %} is faster than using {% include %}&rdquo;, I wonder if it&rsquo;s a big difference and if so why</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="template-caching-might-be-important">template caching might be important</h3>
<p>I think one thing I&rsquo;m learning about Django is that because it&rsquo;s a Framework
(tm), it&rsquo;s easy to accidentally misconfigure it. For example, when I was
thinking about why my site was slow just now, I read the
<a href="https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/topics/performance/">django performance docs</a> and I noticed
a comment saying:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Enabling the cached template loader often improves performance drastically, as
it avoids compiling each template every time it needs to be rendered.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>When I&rsquo;d done CPU profiling I&rsquo;d noticed that it was spending a lot of time
rendering templates! Maybe this could help me!</p>
<p>Clicking through the link, I saw that the cached template loader was supposed to
be on by default, but I&rsquo;d turned it off by accident while trying to do something
else. I think this &ldquo;I turned off the cached template loader by default&rdquo;
things is an example of how I still find the django settings file to be pretty
confusing and difficult. I guess I should just be careful when I go in there.</p>
<p>After turning on template caching, it seems like the site can now pretty easily
handle 12 requests per second or so without using all of the CPU. I have not
carefully benchmarked the before and after but it seems like it&rsquo;s made a pretty
big difference.</p>
<p>One thing that&rsquo;s been surprising to me about Django performance is that I&rsquo;ve
always heard the advice &ldquo;if you have a performance problem, check your database
queries! Maybe add an index!&rdquo;. But I&rsquo;ve been running into a variety of
performance issues (like this template caching thing) that are not because of
slow queries, so instead it&rsquo;s been more useful for me so far to start by running
a CPU profile. And since I&rsquo;m using SQLite, any slow database query problem will
show up on the CPU profile anyway.</p>
<p>Anyway I don&rsquo;t want to get too far into site performance. Like I said it&rsquo;s easy
for me to get interested in profiling, but actually I know a lot about profiling
and it&rsquo;s not the most important thing for me to learn about.</p>
<h3 id="that-s-all-for-now">that&rsquo;s all for now!</h3>
<p>I might say more about what I&rsquo;m enjoying (or having a hard time with!) about
Django later. Trying to write some shorter blog posts recently.</p>

---

### [Learning a few things about running SQLite](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/07/17/learning-about-running-sqlite/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! I&rsquo;ve been working on a Django site recently, and I decided to use SQLite
as the database.
